| state-claims.md:11 | `archive/state-claims.md:11` | archive-only | ## Why the existing rule failed |
| state-claims.md:13 | `archive/state-claims.md:13` | archive-only | **Every wrong answer came from a proxy that was cheaper to reach than the truth.** |
| state-claims.md:15 | `archive/state-claims.md:15` | archive-only | \| The claim \| What was read \| Where the truth was \| |
| state-claims.md:17 | `archive/state-claims.md:17` | archive-only | \| "we're on grammy ^1.29.0" \| the package.json range \| `package-lock.json` \| |
| state-claims.md:18 | `archive/state-claims.md:18` | archive-only | \| "21 files can't find grammy" \| tsc's output \| an empty `node_modules` \| |
| state-claims.md:19 | `archive/state-claims.md:19` | archive-only | \| "3 pre-existing test failures" \| the test runner \| the same empty directory \| |
| state-claims.md:20 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:17` | reworded | \| "the Heart's only consumer is a canary" \| the canary file itself \| a grep for call sites \| |
| state-claims.md:21 | `archive/state-claims.md:21` | archive-only | \| "seven things we should build" \| a sense of the repo \| the code \| |
| state-claims.md:22 | `archive/state-claims.md:22` | archive-only | \| "boot-verify passed" \| esbuild's exit code \| which commit it checked out \| |
| state-claims.md:23 | `archive/state-claims.md:23` | archive-only | \| "no new typecheck errors" \| a diff against a baseline \| whether that baseline was current \| |
| state-claims.md:24 | `archive/state-claims.md:24` | archive-only | \| flags set to `true` \| assuming true means true \| the `=== '1'` in the source \| |
| state-claims.md:26 | `archive/state-claims.md:26` | archive-only | package.json was already in context; the lockfile was one more read. tsc volunteered 183 errors; nobody thinks to ask an empty directory whether it is empty. |
| state-claims.md:26 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:3` | reworded | **The proxy always wins on effort, and it is usually right - which is what makes it dangerous. |
| state-claims.md:26 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:3` | reworded | It is wrong exactly when the answer matters.** |
| state-claims.md:31 | `archive/state-claims.md:31` | archive-only | A rule that says "check the lockfile" loses to "package.json is already open". |
| state-claims.md:31 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:17` | reworded | So the rule cannot be about diligence. |
| state-claims.md:31 | `archive/state-claims.md:31` | archive-only | It has to change the cost. |
| state-claims.md:34 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:3` | reworded | ## The rule (behavior-changing) |
| state-claims.md:38 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:5` | reworded | Not "we're on 1.42.0" but "**the lockfile pins** 1.42.0". |
| state-claims.md:38 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:5` | reworded | Not "nothing imports it" but "**a grep for call sites outside the module and its tests found** nothing". |
| state-claims.md:41 | `archive/state-claims.md:41` | archive-only | This is cheap and it is the whole mechanism: naming the source makes a WRONG source visible to the reader, and - more often - visible to yourself as you type it. |
| state-claims.md:41 | `archive/state-claims.md:41` | archive-only | Half of the eight would have died at the moment of writing "the package.json range says we're on...". |
| state-claims.md:46 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:6` | reworded | Use `zao-verify` - it is faster than the proxy.** |
| state-claims.md:49 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:6` | reworded | zao-verify dep <name>      installed version vs the range vs what is on disk |
| state-claims.md:50 | `archive/state-claims.md:50` | archive-only | zao-verify wired <module>  what actually imports it, excluding itself and tests |
| state-claims.md:51 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:6` | reworded | zao-verify flag <NAME>     the literal the code accepts - `true` is not universal |
| state-claims.md:52 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:6` | reworded | zao-verify exists <term>   what already covers this, before building a second one |
| state-claims.md:53 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:6` | reworded | zao-verify env             is the toolchain installed at ALL |
| state-claims.md:56 | `archive/state-claims.md:56` | archive-only | Every answer prints its source. |
| state-claims.md:56 | `archive/state-claims.md:56` | archive-only | If a question is not covered, name the source by hand - the naming is the requirement, the tool is the convenience. |
| state-claims.md:59 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:7` | reworded | Before believing ANY tool, confirm the tool can run.** |
| state-claims.md:61 | `archive/state-claims.md:61` | archive-only | `zao-verify env` first, whenever a tool reports something surprising. |
| state-claims.md:61 | `archive/state-claims.md:61` | archive-only | 183 typecheck errors on a healthy repo is not a finding, it is a missing `node_modules` - and it cost a day of "no NEW errors" instead of "no errors". |
| state-claims.md:65 | `archive/state-claims.md:65` | archive-only | Measuring a DELTA instead of an ABSOLUTE means you have already adapted to a broken instrument.** "No new errors", "same failures as main", "no new warnings" - each is legitimate ONLY once you have established what th... |
| state-claims.md:65 | `archive/state-claims.md:65` | archive-only | Otherwise the baseline is where the real problems live. |
| state-claims.md:65 | `archive/state-claims.md:65` | archive-only | And re-derive the baseline against the CURRENT target; one captured before an intervening merge invents phantom deltas. |
| state-claims.md:72 | `archive/state-claims.md:72` | archive-only | "Merged" is not "running".** A feature has four independent states - BUILT, WIRED, FLAGGED, LIVE - and only the first is visible from the code. |
| state-claims.md:72 | `archive/state-claims.md:72` | archive-only | A flag set to a value the code rejects reads as done and does nothing, which is worse than unset. |
| state-claims.md:77 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:11` | reworded | ## Silence is not evidence (added 2026-08-08) |

| state-claims.md:79 | `archive/state-claims.md:79` | archive-only | The follow-on, learned the hard way: |
| state-claims.md:79 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:13` | reworded | **you cannot conclude a feature did NOT run from the absence of log lines - unless you first prove it CAN log.** |
| state-claims.md:83 | `archive/state-claims.md:83` | archive-only | Asked which of eight features shipped that week had executed in production, seven days of journald could not answer. |
| state-claims.md:83 | `archive/state-claims.md:83` | archive-only | Not because they had failed. |
| state-claims.md:83 | `archive/state-claims.md:83` | archive-only | Because five of the eight contained **no logging statement of any kind**, and the other three spoke only from inside a `catch`. |
| state-claims.md:83 | `archive/state-claims.md:83` | archive-only | So silence meant "nothing crashed", never "it ran", and a flag reading ON proved the code was REACHABLE, not that it had been reached. |
| state-claims.md:90 | `archive/state-claims.md:90` | archive-only | That gap is the whole distance between merged and running, and it is why a week of work was untestable rather than broken. |
| state-claims.md:93 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:13` | reworded | ### The fix, and why it is one line per feature |
| state-claims.md:95 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:13` | reworded | `featureRan(name, detail)` prints ONE line the first time a feature actually executes after a boot: |
| state-claims.md:98 | `archive/state-claims.md:98` | archive-only | [zoe/ran] dm-build - chat 12345 |
| state-claims.md:100 | `archive/state-claims.md:100` | archive-only | Once per process, not per call, deliberately - a lock acquired every tick would produce thousands of lines a day and become the noise nobody greps (`noisy-signal-guard.md`). |
| state-claims.md:100 | `archive/state-claims.md:100` | archive-only | One line per boot answers the only question being asked, and the whole inventory becomes one grep. |
| state-claims.md:105 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:13` | reworded | Placement is on the SUCCESS path, at the point of effect. |
| state-claims.md:105 | `archive/state-claims.md:105` | archive-only | Never at import time: that proves the module loaded, which is the same empty proof a flag already gives. |
| state-claims.md:109 | `archive/state-claims.md:109` | archive-only | ### What it bought, the same day |
| state-claims.md:111 | `archive/state-claims.md:111` | archive-only | A build request typed into the DM did nothing. |
| state-claims.md:111 | `archive/state-claims.md:111` | archive-only | Zero `[zoe/ran]` lines for the classifier proved it had **never executed** - separating "ran and declined" from "never reached". |
| state-claims.md:111 | `archive/state-claims.md:111` | archive-only | Without that distinction the debugging starts inside the classifier, where nothing was wrong; the actual bug was a different handler eating the message a thousand lines earlier (`first-handler-wins.md`). |

| state-claims.md:123 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:13` | reworded | **Is the log line on the success path, or only in a `catch`?** A module that |

| state-claims.md:211 | `archive/state-claims.md:211` | archive-only | `as of YYYY-MM-DD` is four words and it converts a future wrong answer into a visible one. |
| state-claims.md:213 | `.claude/rules/state-claims.md:23` | reworded | **When you read a time-relative claim, resolve it against when it was |

| state-claims.md:214 | `archive/state-claims.md:214` | archive-only | If you cannot establish when it was written, that is itself the finding - say so rather than assuming it is current. |

| state-claims.md:218 | `archive/state-claims.md:218` | archive-only | contradiction if the 25th is a Tuesday, and `date -j -f %Y-%m-%d` settles it in one command. |
| state-claims.md:218 | `archive/state-claims.md:218` | archive-only | Two of the five above were catchable this way alone. |

| vanishing-dependencies.md:29 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:29` | archive-only | ## The structural cause, named |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:31 | `.claude/rules/vanishing-dependencies.md:3` | reworded | **`~/bin` is a symlink into `~/zaal-dotfiles/bin`, and `~/.claude/skills` into `~/zaal-dotfiles/claude/skills`.** Both are git working trees whose HEAD moves. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:31 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:31` | archive-only | So a branch checkout in a dotfiles repo can break every hook, cron and skill on the machine, and `~/.claude/settings.json` is a symlink into the same tree. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:36 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:36` | archive-only | Runtime dependencies resolving through a mutable working tree is the bug. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:36 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:36` | archive-only | Every instance above is a consequence of it. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:39 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:39` | archive-only | ## The rule (behavior-changing) |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:41 | `.claude/rules/vanishing-dependencies.md:5` | reworded | If a hook, cron, skill, or another script depends on a file, that file is git-tracked. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:41 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:41` | archive-only | No exceptions, and "I'll commit it later" is how all four of these died.** Write it, commit it, in the same pass. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:41 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:41` | archive-only | An untracked file inside a git tree is the worst of both worlds: it looks safe because it is in a repo, and git cannot give it back. |
| vanishing-dependencies.md:47 | `archive/vanishing-dependencies.md:47` | archive-only | Before switching branches in a repo that `~/bin` or `~/.claude` resolves through, know what you are about to change underneath every running session.** `git checkout` in `~/zaal-dotfiles` is not a local action. |

Prove the thing can still run before concluding anything from its quie... |

A backup nobody h... |

| worktree-handoff.md:16 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:16` | archive-only | Every session shared `~/Documents/ZAO OS V1`, so whichever ran `git checkout` last won. |
| worktree-handoff.md:16 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:16` | archive-only | A session wrote doc 548, pushed, then found its own file gone: another session had checked out a different branch beneath it. |
| worktree-handoff.md:16 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:16` | archive-only | `git branch -a` is global, but HEAD is per-worktree, and the main dir is one worktree. |
| worktree-handoff.md:22 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:22` | archive-only | **A spawned lane agent committing into the caller's branch** (2026-08-27, |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:3` | reworded | issue #3338). |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:23` | archive-only | `/meeting` Phase 3.5 spawned a lane agent and handed it a packet path inside the caller's live worktree. |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:23` | archive-only | The lane wrote its review there and committed to the caller's branch on its own. |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:23` | archive-only | The commit was **well-behaved**: one file, no push, no recap edit. |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:23` | archive-only | That is exactly what hid it - nothing failed, and `git status` was clean because the agent had committed its own change. |
| worktree-handoff.md:23 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:23` | archive-only | Two agents were writing the same file in the same window; the race was won by timing, not design. |

| worktree-handoff.md:35 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:7` | reworded | ## The rule |
| worktree-handoff.md:40 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:7` | reworded | subagent, or lane.** Copy the input to a scratch path outside any git tree you are working in, let the other agent write its output there, and copy the result back yourself. |
| worktree-handoff.md:40 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:40` | archive-only | Then the failure cannot occur, rather than being asked not to. |
| worktree-handoff.md:43 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:8` | reworded | - **Telling the other agent not to commit is not enough on its own.** It relies |
| worktree-handoff.md:44 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:44` | archive-only | on compliance, and the compliant case is the one that hides best. |
| worktree-handoff.md:44 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:8` | reworded | If you must rely on it, say in the handoff that the path belongs to another lane, put the instruction in every layer the agent actually reads, and **check it** (below). |
| worktree-handoff.md:47 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:9` | reworded | - **Before spawning, ask: whose tree is the path I am about to hand over?** If |
| worktree-handoff.md:48 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:48` | archive-only | the answer is "mine", you are about to create one of these. |
| worktree-handoff.md:50 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:50` | archive-only | `git status`.** A clean tree does not mean nobody else wrote; it can mean somebody else already committed. |
| worktree-handoff.md:53 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:53` | archive-only | ## Two layers, and they are not alternatives |
| worktree-handoff.md:55 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:55` | archive-only | **Prevention: copy-out.** Removes the possibility. |
| worktree-handoff.md:55 | `.claude/rules/worktree-handoff.md:7` | reworded | The other agent never holds a writable path in your tree, so there is nothing to comply with. |

| worktree-handoff.md:58 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:58` | archive-only | Its own comment states the case better than a rule can: |

| worktree-handoff.md:68 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:68` | archive-only | - **It reports, never reverts.** It cannot tell which pane authored what, and it |
| worktree-handoff.md:69 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:69` | archive-only | says so rather than guessing. |
| worktree-handoff.md:69 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:69` | archive-only | Naming the commits and letting a human look beats a confident wrong rollback. |
| worktree-handoff.md:71 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:71` | archive-only | - **It costs one `git rev-parse`.** There is no budget argument against it. |
| worktree-handoff.md:73 | `archive/worktree-handoff.md:73` | archive-only | The same commit also hardened the instruction side, and how it did that is the transferable part: the no-git rule is repeated in the packet, in the pane brief, AND in the spawn command line, because *a pane acts on th... |
